Lauren Hirsch of *The New York Times* observed that Musk would face financial challenges in owning Twitter, including the company's difficulty in turning a profit.[^cite_note-373] Richard Waters of the *Financial Times* echoed Hirsch's sentiments, adding that it would difficult for Musk to allow the right amount of free speech on the platform.[^cite_note-374] Kate Ferguson of [Deutsche Welle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Welle) viewed Musk as unreliable and therefore unfit to operate Twitter,[^cite_note-375] and Hamilton Nolan of *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* felt the purchase was an attempt by Musk to "control the conversation".[^cite_note-376] Barbara Ortutay, Tom Krisher and Matt O'Brien of the [Associated Press](18935732.md) noted Musk's contradictory and vague messages in the past regarding his vision for Twitter,[^cite_note-377] while Ben Burgis of *Jacobin* criticized liberals' [technocratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technocratic) views and their downplaying of the importance of free speech in response to the purchase, though he remarked that Musk had a history of suppressing his critics.[^cite_note-378] Also writing for *The New York Times*, [Kate Conger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Conger), [Ryan Mac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Mac), and Tiffany Hsu noted that Musk's meetings with civil rights activists and his plans for a content moderation council were reminiscent of the actions taken by Zuckerberg following backlash on Facebook's handling of the [2016 U.S. elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_U.S._elections).[^cite_note-379] *[Politico](9172097.md)*'s [Jack Shafer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Shafer) noted that media coverage of Musk's purchase foretold "chaos, greater political oversight, and outright failure."[^cite_note-Doomed-380]

[Edward Niedermeyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Niedermeyer), an author and critic of Musk, argued that Twitter may fail due to Musk's hubris.[^cite_note-Doomed-380] Scholars who studied the [First Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Amendment), such as professor [Jonathan Turley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Turley) of the [George Washington University Law School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington_University_Law_School), praised Musk's plans to tone down content moderation on Twitter.[^cite_note-Supporters-306] New York University's Center for Business and Human Rights deputy director Paul M. Barrett speculated that a moderation council under Musk would face skepticism due to Musk's "erratic and imperious" behavior in the past.[^cite_note-HateSpeech-198] [Alex Stamos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Stamos) of [Stanford University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_University)'s [Center for International Security and Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_International_Security_and_Cooperation) questioned how Musk would respond to foreign governments attempting to influence Twitter's userbase.[^cite_note-HateSpeech-198] Those in the cryptocurrency field, such as podcaster Bryce Paul and investor [Cathie Wood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathie_Wood), expressed excitement at the prospects of Musk's ownership.[^cite_note-Supporters-306]

Media Matters for America president Angela Carusone warned that Musk's leadership would lead to an increase in disinformation, conspiracy theories, and harassment on Twitter.[^cite_note-FirstDay-381] Literary group [PEN America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PEN_America) opined that the midterm elections in November would serve as a test as to whether Musk would allow disinformation to spread rapidly on the platform,[^cite_note-382] while American left-wing activist coalition Stop the Deal warned of real-world consequences if a rise in hate speech occurs on Twitter.[^cite_note-ClosureReactions2-302][^cite_note-HateSpeech-198] Over 40 civil rights groups signed an open letter to 20 Twitter advertisers urging them to abandon the platform if Musk lifts its content moderation measures.[^cite_note-Exodus-186] Anti-Defamation League CEO [Jonathan Greenblatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Greenblatt) said he was "cautiously optimistic" about the purchase, but expressed concerns over a potential rise in hate speech.[^cite_note-FirstDay-381] [Fight for the Future](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fight_for_the_Future) director [Evan Greer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evan_Greer) appreciated Musk's idea for a moderation council, but noted that Musk had supreme authority on any decisions made.[^cite_note-Imprint-195] [Eliot Higgins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eliot_Higgins) of [Bellingcat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellingcat) believed that Musk's attempts to promote free speech would backfire under increased government regulation.[^cite_note-ClosureReactions3-295] LGBTQ+ advocacy group [Human Rights Campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Campaign) voiced concern at Twitter's new ownership and argued that Twitter has the responsibility to stop its platform from turning into a "dangerous media environment".[^cite_note-HateSpeech-198] The Guardian has also suspended submissions to X due to a number of far-right conspiracy theories and posts promoting racism.[^cite_note-383]

## Legacy

On the first anniversary of the acquisition, Musk valued the company at \$19 billion, a 55 percent decrease from the buyout's \$44 billion purchase price.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] [Fidelity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidelity_Investments), which contributed \$300 million to the acquisition, depressed the value by 65 percent.[^cite_note-386] Statistics indicated a 30 percent decline in active users, 60 percent decline in advertising,[^cite_note-387] 14 percent decline in website traffic,[^cite_note-Husk-388] and 38 percent decline in app downloads.[^cite_note-389] The company has denied this, claiming an increase in engagement.[^cite_note-Husk-388] A [nonfiction book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonfiction_book) about the acquisition, *Breaking Twitter: Elon Musk and the Most Controversial Corporate Takeover in History*, was announced by author [Ben Mezrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Mezrich) in December 2022 and released in November 2023.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391]

Many publications reflected on Twitter's first year under Musk's ownership. Journalists for *The Washington Post*, citing data from the [University of Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Washington), wrote that the platform had "become a cacophony of misinformation and confusing reports", remarking on Twitter's stark shift toward conservatism.[^cite_note-392] Pranav Dixit of *The Guardian* lambasted the changes and reforms Musk had instituted in the past year,[^cite_note-393] while Miles Klee of *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone)* criticized Musk's alleged pandering to "[right-wing extremists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-wing_extremists) and peddlers of misinformation".[^cite_note-394] *The Verge*'s Jay Peters pointed to Musk's radical changes and declining user numbers as evidence that the takeover was a "disaster",[^cite_note-395] while Bloomberg's Aisha Counts zeroed in on the increase in misinformation and hate speech.[^cite_note-396] Kate Conger of *The New York Times* and Alex Kirshner of *[Slate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slate_\(magazine\))* lamented that Musk's acquisition had fundamentally changed the meaning of Twitter.[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398] Annika Burgess of the [Australian Broadcasting Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Broadcasting_Corporation) declared, "Twitter as the world knew it is dead".[^cite_note-399] Musk has largely dismissed this negative commentary.[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402]

*The Wall Street Journal* opined in 2024 that the \$13 billion Musk had borrowed to buy Twitter was "the worst merger-finance deal for banks" since the [2008 financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_financial_crisis), adding that the "allure" of banking Musk had proved "too attractive to pass up".[^cite_note-403] *The Washington Post* reported that the company had lost \$24 billion in equity value, "a vaporization of wealth that has little parallel outside the realm of economic or industry-specific crashes, or devastating corporate scandals."[^cite_note-404]

### Class action lawsuit and outcome

A class action suit was filed against Musk just before he bought Twitter.[^cite_note-lat-jury-finds-elon-musk-misled-investors-during-twitter-purchase-405] On March 20, 2026, the [San Francisco, California](49728.md) jury found Musk liable for misleading investors, though they absolved him of some fraud allegations, finding he did not "scheme" to defraud investors.[^cite_note-misleadverdict-406][^cite_note-lat-jury-finds-elon-musk-misled-investors-during-twitter-purchase-405] Specifically, Musk was found to have misled shareholders in two tweets, but not in a podcast, as the views he expressed in that were an opinion.[^cite_note-misleadverdict-406][^cite_note-lat-jury-finds-elon-musk-misled-investors-during-twitter-purchase-405] Damages have yet to be calculated, but plaintiffs' lawyers estimated them to be \$2.5 billion.[^cite_note-407]
